[00:07:17] Good afternoon, everyone. Madam Chair, thank you, and the members of the Board of County Supervisors. Since the early 1900s, Prince William County government has hosted Black History Month events and activities to celebrate the diversity of this county in the United States. Each year, the county's observance adopts the national theme of the Association of African-American Life and History.

[00:07:37] This organization was founded long ago, and it also founded Black History Month. It takes as its mission to promote, research, preserve, interpret, and disseminate information about the history, culture, and lives of African Americans and their experience in the global community.

[00:07:52] This year's theme, as I said before, is Black Resistance. It examines how black Americans have fought oppression from the inception of the creation of this country. Throughout American history, this resistance has come in many forms, culminating as a continuous movement to obtain freedom and equality.

[00:08:10] Some of the earliest black resistance came during the Middle Passage when some slaves defied captivity by sacrificing themselves to the sea by jumping overboard during the transatlantic voyage to America. As resistance continued, many well-known historical figures such as Harriet Tubman, Frederick Douglass, Sojourner Truth, Dred Scott, and Nat Turner opposed the inhumane treatment of blacks in America.

[00:08:32] Other lesser-known activists such as Margaret Garner, who actually killed her daughter rather than allow the child to be returned to slavery, Bishop Richard Allen, the founder of the African Methodist Episcopal Church, 15-year-old Claudette Colvin, who refused to give up her seat on a bus to a white man nine months before the incident that we know as Rosa Parks, Elizabeth Freeman, who became the first African-American woman to successfully file a lawsuit for freedom in the state of Massachusetts Beinard Rushton a black gay man was a key advisor to

[00:09:11] Reverend Dr. Martin Luther King who helped organize the Montgomery bus boycott and was architect of the March on Washington and a whole host of others these people personified justice sacrifice dignity persistence and freedom the struggle for equality did I end with introduction to the 13 14 to 15 amendments of the constitution however at the end of reconstruction African Americans faced an onslaught of violence and new challenges in racism, Jim Crow, KKK, and government sponsored white supremacy and segregation, which all worked consistently to keep African Americans

[00:09:46] excluded, uneducated, financially underprivileged, and incarcerated. A new period of black resistance had arisen. New leaders emerged in this freer and different social landscape. In addition to the traditional activists of the past, black authors, and sports and entertainment celebrities merged as leaders as the social consciousness of African Americans became more sophisticated. Writers such as James Baldwin, Zora Neale Hurston, and Langston Hughes influenced the civil rights movement by creatively narrating the African American experience through expressing their antipathy towards inequality. Cassius Clay, who changed his name to Muhammad Ali and protested the

[00:10:24] mistreatment of African Americans in the United States and involvement in the Vietnam War. Tommy Smith and John Carlos famously raised their fists during the medal ceremony at the 1968 Mexico City Olympics. Harry Balafonte, Cicely Tyson, Ozzie Davis, and Sidney Poitier used their Hollywood influence to become prolific, outspoken ambassadors for freedom and equality. [00:11:00] harmoniously reminded us of who we are and how we should feel about ourselves in his song, Say It Loud, Black and I'm Proud. [00:12:13] Prince William County Human Rights Commission. [02:11:51] This proffer does not do that. In addition, the applicant has also proffered a limit to prominent discrete noise, also known as tonal noise. This is like a constant hum noise and is required to be three decibels less.

[02:12:09] Prominent discrete noise is an instance when one-third octave band sound pressure level and the band with the tone exceeds the arrhythmic average of the sound pressure levels of the two contiguous one-third octave bands. So very technical, but essentially, it's a constant humming noise.

[02:12:26] The applicant has also proffered to provide sound studies to be conducted and reviewed during the site plan stage to confirm that the sound measure limits can be accommodated. [03:05:44] Thank you, Supervisor Bodie. I can talk generally about the proffers, but if you have any specific sound technical questions, we have Scott Harvey with Phoenix Acoustics with us. So the way the proffers were set up, you know, the biggest issue we had, or the county had, When we were deferred in September, the Great Oaks situation had just really started taking off, and people really became concerned about those facilities, and a couple of things became obvious.

[03:06:22] The county didn't have HVAC systems included in their noise ordinance because they didn't want homeowners calling the police on each other when their compressors outside made noise during the summer months or whatever. And secondly, even if it was included, the ability to enforce the noise ordinance is actually quite limited.

[03:06:44] So I worked with Planning Commissioner Gordy and a number of other folks, both from the industry and both from our acoustical engineering firm, to understand how to create a standard for developing data centers in areas that are transitioned between your residential areas and your industrial uses, which this property is.

[03:07:05] If you look at this property on one side, it's clearly data centers. On the other side, it's across the street and adjacent, not just across the street, but also adjacent to some residential homes and communities. So what do we do in those areas, and how do we prevent noise from being a problem for them?

[03:07:21] So we started off by understanding the problems that they were having at Great Oaks, which were driven a lot by equipment, which is the biggest driver of noise, and site layout. So even if your equipment's not necessarily the loudest, if it's oriented towards other equipment that makes similar noise and it bangs into it, the noise effectively bangs into each other and channels and creates the problems they have, that's a site design issue. So we decided that we would proffer, prior to the issuance of a site development permit, before any construction takes place on the site, that we would do an in-depth analysis of the sound for the project

[03:07:59] that it talks about, that analyzes both the site layout as well as the type of equipment used in its construction to determine how, from an engineering standpoint and a proper planning standpoint, you can ensure that you won't have the noise issues at the property line into other uses.

[03:08:17] Secondly, when you get to actually constructing a building and getting it fully operational, you have the applicant is required to come back and do a secondary study of those buildings at completion with each building. So, you know, we're talking about a technology park here, which is a number of different buildings.

[03:08:35] And I know there's a lot of concerns about additive noise and what that means and what it doesn't mean, but at the property line with the residential uses, we have to go back and measure every time a new building comes online to ensure that we are in conformance with the noise study that was produced at the onset of the project.

[03:08:53] Similarly, we took it a step further, and that's been the conversation about what exactly happens if there's a problem and how often you do studies and whether or not, you know, if the developer is using their own noise engineer. The issue is it's extraordinarily technical, so we set it up to where the applicant can present the noise study findings and bear the cost of all those noise studies, And the county has the ability to approve the consultant.

[03:09:27] So it's not just a fox in the hen house type of situation, so to speak. Did that answer your question? It does. It answers sort of the first two segments I wanted to have you guys speak to, I guess. So the current proffers talk about site plans, but it sounds to me like you're going to be doing a little bit more granular than that, where as each building is built, there's going to be a subsequent study.

[03:09:48] Is that correct? Correct. There's the first one that's done prior to site plan approval that sets the assumptions for, like I said, building orientation and equipment. You can deal with it from an engineering standpoint. The secondary or follow-up sound studies are there only to make sure that you met the obligations that were outlined in the original study.

[03:10:31] So sound barriers are generally 20, 25 feet, 30 feet, whatever. It'll be driven by the actual sound study to determine how high they need to be. [06:25:11] Your adrenal glands are pumping stress hormones. Your blood pressure and heart rate are rising. Your digestion is slowing down. Your brain continues to process sounds while you snooze. [06:25:34] Experts say your body does not adapt to noise. Large-scale studies show that if the din keeps up over days, months, years, Noise exposure increases your risk of high blood pressure, coronary heart disease, heart attacks, strokes, diabetes, dementia, and depression.

[06:25:54] Children suffer not only physically, there was a study 18 months after a new airport opened in Munich, the blood pressure and stress hormone levels of neighboring children soared, but also behaviorally and cognitively as well.

[06:26:12] A landmark study published in 1975 found that reading scores of sixth graders whose classroom faced a clattering subway lagged nearly a year behind the students in quieter classrooms, a difference that disappeared once the soundproofing materials were installed.

[06:26:31] I don't want to reiterate everything else that everyone has said. We need to just say no to the data centers or at least defer the decision until we can perform these studies to underscore that these conditions won't be present for residents and students in our county.
